Study its loan/send API and pool-offset\nmachinery before implementing the pools — its lessons are\ncheaper stolen than rediscovered.\n\nWhat keeps this project distinct from it:\n\n- iceoryx2 is service-oriented middleware (discovery, naming,\n  configuration) for Linux/macOS/Windows/QNX-class platforms;\n  this crate is a small set of composable primitives.\n- No `no_std` / load-store-only floor — our ring reaches\n  `thumbv6m`, with CAS confined to the pool layer.\n- Trust posture: our attach-validated\n  hostile-peer-cannot-cause-UB discipline versus cooperating\n  participants within a framework.\n\n### Open questions"
}

The design\ndoc gains a \"Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues\"\nsection; todo.md gains the matching ranked entries and Ideas.\n\n- Architecture: the ring stays the unchanged primitive;\n  pools own message memory, queues carry `(pool id, offset)`\n  descriptors, provenance travels in a per-buffer header.\n- Requirements stated up front: any quantity of queues and\n  pools, any message over any queue, inter- and intra-process\n  zero-copy (offsets only, no pointers in shared structures),\n  heterogeneous attach-validated pools, SPSC now / MPSC\n  later, single allocator now / shared allocation eventually.\n- Free path: intrusive LIFO free-stack (single-popper\n  Treiber — no ABA; validated pops). We think LIFO beats a\n  free-ring on working-set size. First CAS in the design;\n  the ring protocol stays load/store-only.\n- Overflow FIFO (future): ring-Full sends append to a\n  sender-private intrusive pending list through the same\n  embedded next-link; naturally bounded by pool capacity, so\n  backpressure reappears as allocation failure.\n- Prior art: iceoryx2 covers most of this layer as std\n  middleware; recorded what to study and what keeps this\n  project distinct (no_std floor, hostile-peer posture,\n  primitives-not-framework).\n\n# References"

```
vc-x1 0.67.0

Dual-repo commit+push+finalize in one resumable command.

Collapses today's manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow into a
single subcommand with two interactive approval gates and a
state machine with persistent progress so interruptions can
resume without re-doing completed stages.

Stages: preflight (fmt/clippy/test) → review (approve diff)
→ message ($EDITOR / --title+--body, approve text) →
commit-app → commit-claude (skipped if clean) → bookmark-both
→ push-app → finalize-claude. Failures in commit-app /
commit-claude / bookmark-both roll both repos back via
`jj op restore` to the snapshot recorded before commit-app.
After push-app succeeds the remote boundary is crossed and
recovery is forward-only.

Non-interactive use: pass both --title and --body plus --yes
to skip the review gate. Saved state carries title/body
across resumes so only the first invocation needs them.

Usage: vc-x1 push [OPTIONS] [BOOKMARK]

Arguments:
  [BOOKMARK]
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (positional form of `--bookmark`).
          
          Accepting a positional lets the common case read as `vc-x1 push main`
          without the `--bookmark` ceremony; `--bookmark` is kept as an alias
          for scripts and for composition with other args. The two forms
          conflict if both supplied.

Options:
      --bookmark <BOOKMARK>
          Bookmark to advance in both repos (flag form; see positional)

  -V, --version
          Print the `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` banner as the first line, then continue. With
          no subcommand, prints the banner and exits.
          
          Replaces clap's auto-version (which would exit after printing): the
          banner now rides along with normal subcommand execution rather than
          gating it, so scripts can capture the version *and* the command's
          output in one invocation.

      --restart
          Clear any saved state file and start from stage 1

  -v, --verbose...
          Verbose output: -v debug, -vv trace

      --from <STAGE>
          Explicit stage to jump to (advanced / debug use)

          Possible values:
          - preflight:       Run fmt / clippy / test / install / retest
          - review:          Present diff for the first approval gate
          - message:         Compose / edit the commit message; present for
            second gate
          - commit-app:      Commit the app repo
          - commit-claude:   Commit the `.claude` session repo (skipped if
            empty)
          - bookmark-both:   Advance both bookmarks to `@-`
          - push-app:        `jj git push --bookmark <b> -R .`
          - finalize-claude: `vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash --push <b>
            ...`

      --log <LOG>
          Log file path (captures all levels)

      --step
          Pause between every stage for an interactive approval gate

      --status
          Print where the saved state thinks we are and exit

      --recheck
          Re-run `preflight` even on resume (default: skip if last run
          succeeded)

      --no-finalize
          Stop before `finalize-claude` so it can be run manually

      --dry-run
          Print the exact commands for every stage without side effects

      --title <STR>
          Commit title (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

      --body <STR>
          Commit body (skip `$EDITOR` for the message stage)

  -y, --yes
          Auto-approve interactive prompts (review gate, message-edit
          confirmation). Required when `--title` / `--body` aren't both supplied
          in non-interactive contexts

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')
```

```
1	//! Zero-copy no_std SPSC ring buffer over a caller-provided
2	//! memory region, per [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]:
3	//!
4	//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`Header`] spanning four cache lines
5	//!   (immutable geometry, producer index, consumer index, app
6	//!   user words), followed by M slots of N bytes.
7	//! - Indices are free-running `AtomicU32`s, masked only at slot
8	//!   access; full is `p - c == M`, no sacrificial slot.
9	//! - Messages move in place: the producer writes through a
10	//!   [`WriteSlot`] `&mut T`, the consumer reads through a
11	//!   [`ReadSlot`] `&T` — zerocopy traits bound `T`, no
12	//!   serialization step. Each side reserves at most one slot at
13	//!   a time; the guard holds the endpoint borrow until commit /
14	//!   release (or drop).
15	//!
16	//! [notes/ring-buffer-design.md]: https://github.com/winksaville/zc-ring-x1/blob/main/notes/ring-buffer-design.md
17	
18	#![cfg_attr(not(test), no_std)]
19	
20	use core::marker::PhantomData;
21	use core::mem::{align_of, size_of};
22	use core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};
23	
24	mod consumer;
25	mod producer;
26	
27	pub use consumer::{Consumer, Empty, ReadSlot};
28	pub use producer::{Full, Producer, WriteSlot};
29	
30	/// Cache-line size the layout is built around.
31	///
32	/// - Slot size must be a multiple of this; slots and the region
33	///   itself must be aligned to it.
34	pub const CACHE_LINE: usize = 64;
35	
36	/// Layout marker written by [`Ring::init`]; rejects foreign
37	/// regions on attach.
38	const MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5231; // "ZCR1"
39	
40	/// Bumped on any change to the region layout.
41	const LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 2;
42	
43	/// Number of `AtomicU32` words in the header's app-owned
44	/// `user` line.
45	pub const USER_WORDS: usize = 16;
46	
47	/// Cache-line-aligned wrapper granting its field sole
48	/// ownership of the line.
49	///
50	/// - `repr(align(N))` accepts only an integer literal — it
51	///   cannot name [`CACHE_LINE`] — so the `64` is written out
52	///   and a const assert ties them back together.
53	#[repr(C, align(64))]
54	struct CacheAligned<T>(T);
55	
56	const _: () = assert!(align_of::<CacheAligned<AtomicU32>>() == CACHE_LINE);
57	
58	impl<T> core::ops::Deref for CacheAligned<T> {
59	    type Target = T;
60	    /// Access the wrapped value.
61	    fn deref(&self) -> &T {
62	        &self.0
63	    }
64	}
65	
66	/// Control block at offset 0 of the region — one struct spanning
67	/// four cache lines so each concurrently-written index owns its
68	/// own line.
69	///
70	/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Ring::init`] with `magic`
71	///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter. Cold: per-op
72	///   paths use the handles' snapshots.
73	/// - line 1: `producer_idx`, written only by the producer.
74	/// - line 2: `consumer_idx`, written only by the consumer.
75	/// - line 3: `user`, app-owned scratch — last, so an app
76	///   overrun walks into its own slot data, not an index line.
77	/// - Every field is atomic: the region may be mapped by a peer
78	///   at any time, so even "immutable" fields must be free of
79	///   data races. `AtomicU32` has the same layout as `u32`, so
80	///   this does not affect `layout_version`.
81	#[repr(C)]
82	pub struct Header {
83	    /// Layout marker ([`MAGIC`]); stored last by init
84	    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`), so a
85	    /// peer that observes it also observes the geometry.
86	    magic: AtomicU32,
87	    /// Layout version ([`LAYOUT_VERSION`]).
88	    layout_version: AtomicU32,
89	    /// Slot size N in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.
90	    slot_size: AtomicU32,
91	    /// Slot count M — a power of two `<= 2^31`.
92	    capacity: AtomicU32,
93	    /// [`CACHE_LINE`] this region was built with. The line size
94	    /// is a layout parameter (padding, offsets, slot granule),
95	    /// so builds must agree; attach validates it like the rest
96	    /// of the geometry.
97	    cache_line: AtomicU32,
98	    /// Free-running count of messages committed.
99	    producer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
100	    /// Free-running count of messages released.
101	    consumer_idx: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,
102	    /// App-owned scratch line ([`USER_WORDS`] words): zeroed by
103	    /// init, then never read, written, or interpreted by the
104	    /// crate. See the design doc's "Blocking and user words".
105	    user: CacheAligned<[AtomicU32; USER_WORDS]>,
106	}
107	
108	const _: () = assert!(size_of::<Header>() == 4 * CACHE_LINE);
109	
110	/// Errors from [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`] region
111	/// validation.
112	#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
113	pub enum Error {
114	    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned.
115	    Misaligned,
116	    /// Region is smaller than header + slots.
117	    TooSmall,
118	    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.
119	    BadSlotSize,
120	    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.
121	    BadCapacity,
122	    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a ring region.
123	    BadMagic,
124	    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.
125	    BadLayoutVersion,
126	    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE`].
127	    BadCacheLine,
128	}
129	
130	/// A validated view over a ring region; split into the two
131	/// endpoint handles with [`Ring::split`].
132	///
133	/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at
134	///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer
135	///   could scribble on, so a hostile peer can degrade the ring
136	///   to garbage messages or spurious Full/Empty, never to UB.
137	pub struct Ring<'a> {
138	    /// The region's control block.
139	    header: &'a Header,
140	    /// Base of the slot array (offset `size_of::<Header>()`).
141	    slots: *mut u8,
142	    /// Snapshot of `header.slot_size`.
143	    slot_size: u32,
144	    /// Snapshot of `header.capacity`.
145	    capacity: u32,
146	    /// Slot-position mask (`capacity - 1`).
147	    mask: u32,
148	    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,
149	}
150	
151	impl<'a> Ring<'a> {
152	    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the ring over it.
153	    ///
154	    /// - `slot_size` — N bytes per slot, a [`CACHE_LINE`]
155	    ///   multiple.
156	    /// - `capacity` — M slots, a power of two `<= 2^31`.
157	    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned and at least
158	    ///   `size_of::<Header>() + M * N` bytes.
159	    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {
160	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
161	        let len = region.len();
162	        // Taken exactly once: a second `as_mut_ptr()` would
163	        // retag the slice and invalidate `header` under Stacked
164	        // Borrows (Miri-verified).
165	        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();
166	        let header = header_ptr(base, len)?;
167	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
168	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
169	        }
170	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
171	        // header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed for 'a;
172	        // any byte pattern is a valid Header (all-atomic
173	        // fields, plain-byte padding).
174	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
175	        header
176	            .layout_version
177	            .store(LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);
178	        header.slot_size.store(slot_size, Ordering::Relaxed);
179	        header.capacity.store(capacity, Ordering::Relaxed);
180	        header
181	            .cache_line
182	            .store(CACHE_LINE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);
183	        header.producer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
184	        header.consumer_idx.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
185	        for word in header.user.iter() {
186	            word.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);
187	        }
188	        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region must
189	        // never observe MAGIC before the geometry it validates.
190	        // (Re-initializing a region a peer is already attached
191	        // to is outside the contract.)
192	        header.magic.store(MAGIC, Ordering::Release);
193	        // SAFETY: in bounds — region.len() >= header + slots.
194	        let slots = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
195	        Ok(Ring {
196	            header,
197	            slots,
198	            slot_size,
199	            capacity,
200	            mask: capacity - 1,
201	            _region: PhantomData,
202	        })
203	    }
204	
205	    /// Attach to a region another process (or an earlier call)
206	    /// already initialized, validating its header.
207	    ///
208	    /// # Safety
209	    ///
210	    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive
211	    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a
212	    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).
213	    /// - No other producer attaches if this side will produce;
214	    ///   likewise for the consumer side (SPSC contract).
215	    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {
216	        let header = header_ptr(region, len)?;
217	        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the Header checked by
218	        // header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is live and
219	        // shared.
220	        let header = unsafe { &*header };
221	        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic: a
222	        // valid magic means the geometry stores are visible.
223	        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != MAGIC {
224	            return Err(Error::BadMagic);
225	        }
226	        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != LAYOUT_VERSION {
227	            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);
228	        }
229	        if header.cache_line.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE as u32 {
230	            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);
231	        }
232	        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read it.
233	        let slot_size = header.slot_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
234	        let capacity = header.capacity.load(Ordering::Relaxed);
235	        validate_geometry(slot_size, capacity)?;
236	        if (len as u64) < region_size(slot_size, capacity) {
237	            return Err(Error::TooSmall);
238	        }
239	        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + slots.
240	        let slots = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<Header>()) };
241	        Ok(Ring {
242	            header,
243	            slots,
244	            slot_size,
245	            capacity,
246	            mask: capacity - 1,
247	            _region: PhantomData,
248	        })
249	    }
250	
251	    /// Split into the producer and consumer endpoint handles.
252	    ///
253	    /// - Consuming `self` makes each handle exist at most once
254	    ///   per ring per process; cross-process, one producing and
255	    ///   one consuming process is the SPSC contract.
256	    pub fn split(self) -> (Producer<'a>, Consumer<'a>) {
257	        (
258	            Producer::new(
259	                self.header,
260	                self.slots,
261	                self.slot_size,
262	                self.capacity,
263	                self.mask,
264	            ),
265	            Consumer::new(self.header, self.slots, self.slot_size, self.mask),
266	        )
267	    }
268	}
269	
270	/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the `Header`
271	/// it must start with; shared by [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
272	///
273	/// - Checks alignment and room for the `Header` itself. The
274	///   full-geometry length check stays with the caller: init
275	///   knows the geometry from its parameters, attach only after
276	///   reading it from the returned header.
277	/// - Returns a raw pointer — no deref here. Each caller
278	///   discharges its own safety obligation (init: exclusive
279	///   borrow; attach: the caller's shared-mapping contract).
280	fn header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const Header, Error> {
281	    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {
282	        return Err(Error::Misaligned);
283	    }
284	    if len < size_of::<Header>() {
285	        return Err(Error::TooSmall);
286	    }
287	    Ok(base as *const Header)
288	}
289	
290	/// Bytes needed for a region with the given geometry.
291	///
292	/// - Computed in u64: the usize product can wrap on 32-bit
293	///   targets (e.g. N = 2^26, M = 2^6), which would validate a
294	///   tiny region and let safe code write out of bounds.
295	fn region_size(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> u64 {
296	    size_of::<Header>() as u64 + slot_size as u64 * capacity as u64
297	}
298	
299	/// Shared geometry checks for [`Ring::init`] / [`Ring::attach`].
300	fn validate_geometry(slot_size: u32, capacity: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {
301	    if slot_size == 0 || !(slot_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {
302	        return Err(Error::BadSlotSize);
303	    }
304	    // The `> 1 << 31` arm is unreachable (u32 powers of two are
305	    // <= 2^31); it stays to document the M bound the occupancy
306	    // math relies on.
307	    if capacity == 0 || !capacity.is_power_of_two() || capacity > 1 << 31 {
308	        return Err(Error::BadCapacity);
309	    }
310	    Ok(())
311	}
312	
313	/// Check `T` fits a slot; called once per `reserve_slot` (both
314	/// endpoints).
315	///
316	/// - Panics on a type-geometry mismatch — that is a programming
317	///   error, not a runtime condition.
318	fn check_type<T>(slot_size: u32) {
319	    assert!(size_of::<T>() <= slot_size as usize, "T larger than slot");
320	    assert!(
321	        core::mem::align_of::<T>() <= CACHE_LINE,
322	        "T alignment exceeds slot alignment"
323	    );
324	}
325	
326	/// Pointer to the slot for free-running index `idx`.
327	///
328	/// - Returned range is `slot_size` bytes, cache-line aligned
329	///   (base is, and slot_size is a line multiple).
330	fn slot_ptr(slots: *mut u8, idx: u32, mask: u32, slot_size: u32) -> *mut u8 {
331	    let pos = (idx & mask) as usize;
332	    // SAFETY: pos < capacity, so the offset is inside the slot
333	    // array validated at init/attach.
334	    unsafe { slots.add(pos * slot_size as usize) }
335	}
336	
337	#[cfg(test)]
338	mod tests {
339	    use super::*;
340	    use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};
341	
342	    /// Test message; two words so a torn write would be visible.
343	    #[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable, Debug, PartialEq)]
344	    #[repr(C)]
345	    struct Msg {
346	        seq: u64,
347	        val: u64,
348	    }
349	
350	    /// Test region size: header + (4 slots × 1 line each).
351	    const REGION_BYTES: usize = size_of::<Header>() + (4 * CACHE_LINE);
352	
353	    /// Cache-line-aligned backing store for the tests' rings.
354	    #[repr(C, align(64))]
355	    struct Region([u8; REGION_BYTES]);
356	
357	    impl Region {
358	        fn new() -> Self {
359	            Region([0; REGION_BYTES])
360	        }
361	    }
362	
363	    #[test]
364	    fn init_rejects_bad_geometry() {
365	        let mut r = Region::new();
366	        assert_eq!(
367	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 63, 4).err().unwrap(),
368	            Error::BadSlotSize
369	        );
370	        assert_eq!(
371	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 0, 4).err().unwrap(),
372	            Error::BadSlotSize
373	        );
374	        assert_eq!(
375	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 3).err().unwrap(),
376	            Error::BadCapacity
377	        );
378	        assert_eq!(
379	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 0).err().unwrap(),
380	            Error::BadCapacity
381	        );
382	        assert_eq!(Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 8).err().unwrap(), Error::TooSmall);
383	        assert_eq!(
384	            Ring::init(&mut r.0[1..], 64, 4).err().unwrap(),
385	            Error::Misaligned
386	        );
387	        // 32-bit tripwire: N * M wraps a 32-bit usize (2^26 *
388	        // 2^6 = 2^32); the u64 region_size must still reject it.
389	        assert_eq!(
390	            Ring::init(&mut r.0, 1 << 26, 1 << 6).err().unwrap(),
391	            Error::TooSmall
392	        );
393	    }
394	
395	    #[test]
396	    fn attach_validates_header() {
397	        let mut r = Region::new();
398	        // Not initialized yet: magic is zero.
399	        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }
400	            .err()
401	            .unwrap();
402	        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadMagic);
403	        Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
404	        let ring = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }.unwrap();
405	        assert_eq!(ring.slot_size, 64);
406	        assert_eq!(ring.capacity, 4);
407	        assert_eq!(ring.mask, 3);
408	        // A region built with a different CACHE_LINE (simulated
409	        // by editing the recorded value) is rejected.
410	        ring.header.cache_line.store(128, Ordering::Relaxed);
411	        let err = unsafe { Ring::attach(r.0.as_mut_ptr(), r.0.len()) }
412	            .err()
413	            .unwrap();
414	        assert_eq!(err, Error::BadCacheLine);
415	    }
416	
417	    #[test]
418	    fn user_words_zeroed_and_shared() {
419	        let mut r = Region::new();
420	        // Dirty the region so init's zeroing is observable.
421	        r.0.fill(0xAA);
422	        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
423	        let (prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
424	        assert!(prod.user().iter().all(|w| w.load(Ordering::Relaxed) == 0));
425	        // Both endpoints see the same line; a producer-side
426	        // store is visible through the consumer's accessor.
427	        prod.user()[0].store(7, Ordering::Release);
428	        assert_eq!(cons.user()[0].load(Ordering::Acquire), 7);
429	        // The user line must not alias ring state: scribbling
430	        // all of it leaves the ring empty and functional.
431	        for w in cons.user().iter() {
432	            w.store(u32::MAX, Ordering::Relaxed);
433	        }
434	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
435	    }
436	
437	    #[test]
438	    fn indices_survive_u32_wrap() {
439	        let mut r = Region::new();
440	        let ring = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap();
441	        // Simulate a long-running ring two commits shy of the
442	        // u32 wrap (empty state: p == c).
443	        let start = u32::MAX - 1;
444	        ring.header.producer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
445	        ring.header.consumer_idx.store(start, Ordering::Relaxed);
446	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = ring.split();
447	
448	        // Fill: producer_idx runs MAX-1, MAX, 0, 1 — masked
449	        // slot positions 2, 3, 0, 1.
450	        for i in 0..4u64 {
451	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
452	            slot.seq = i;
453	            slot.val = i;
454	            slot.commit();
455	        }
456	        assert!(prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
457	
458	        // Drain in order across the wrap.
459	        for i in 0..4u64 {
460	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
461	            assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
462	            msg.release();
463	        }
464	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
465	    }
466	
467	    #[test]
468	    fn roundtrip_full_empty() {
469	        let mut r = Region::new();
470	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
471	
472	        // Empty at start.
473	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
474	
475	        // Fill all 4 slots, then Full.
476	        for i in 0..4u64 {
477	            let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
478	            slot.seq = i;
479	            slot.val = i * 10;
480	            slot.commit();
481	        }
482	        assert!(prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
483	
484	        // Drain in order; wrap: slot 0 is reused by seq 4.
485	        for i in 0..4u64 {
486	            let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
487	            assert_eq!(
488	                *msg,
489	                Msg {
490	                    seq: i,
491	                    val: i * 10
492	                }
493	            );
494	            msg.release();
495	        }
496	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
497	
498	        // One more write lands in the masked-around slot 0.
499	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
500	        slot.seq = 4;
501	        slot.commit();
502	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
503	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 4);
504	        msg.release();
505	    }
506	
507	    #[test]
508	    // Dropping the guards is the behavior under test; they have
509	    // no Drop impl by design (abandon = do nothing).
510	    #[allow(clippy::drop_non_drop)]
511	    fn abandoned_guards_publish_nothing() {
512	        let mut r = Region::new();
513	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
514	
515	        // Reserve then drop: nothing published.
516	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
517	        slot.seq = 99;
518	        drop(slot);
519	        assert!(cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().is_err());
520	
521	        // Commit one, reserve_slot then drop: still unread, the
522	        // next reserve_slot returns it again.
523	        let mut slot = prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
524	        slot.seq = 1;
525	        slot.commit();
526	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
527	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
528	        drop(msg);
529	        let msg = cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
530	        assert_eq!(msg.seq, 1);
531	        msg.release();
532	    }
533	
534	    #[test]
535	    fn threaded_spsc() {
536	        // Reduced under Miri: interpreted spin loops are slow,
537	        // and its scheduler explores interleavings at any count.
538	        const COUNT: u64 = if cfg!(miri) { 200 } else { 100_000 };
539	        let mut r = Region::new();
540	        let (mut prod, mut cons) = Ring::init(&mut r.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();
541	
542	        std::thread::scope(|s| {
543	            s.spawn(move || {
544	                for i in 0..COUNT {
545	                    loop {
546	                        match prod.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
547	                            Ok(mut slot) => {
548	                                slot.seq = i;
549	                                slot.val = i * 3;
550	                                slot.commit();
551	                                break;
552	                            }
553	                            Err(Full) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
554	                        }
555	                    }
556	                }
557	            });
558	            s.spawn(move || {
559	                for i in 0..COUNT {
560	                    loop {
561	                        match cons.reserve_slot::<Msg>() {
562	                            Ok(msg) => {
563	                                assert_eq!(msg.seq, i);
564	                                assert_eq!(msg.val, i * 3);
565	                                msg.release();
566	                                break;
567	                            }
568	                            Err(Empty) => std::hint::spin_loop(),
569	                        }
570	                    }
571	                }
572	            });
573	        });
574	    }
575	}
576

  "content": "//! Message pool: fixed-size buffers over a caller-provided\n//! region, handed out through an intrusive LIFO free-stack —\n//! the allocation half of the messaging layer, decoupling\n//! \"get a message\" from \"send it\" (see the design doc's\n//! \"Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues\").\n//!\n//! - One `#[repr(C)]` [`PoolHeader`] spanning two cache lines\n//!   (immutable geometry, free-stack head), followed by\n//!   `buf_count` buffers of `buf_size` bytes each.\n//! - The free-stack is intrusive with zero per-buffer\n//!   overhead: a *free* buffer's first word holds the index\n//!   of the next free buffer; an allocated buffer carries no\n//!   header at all (pool-id provenance is deferred — see\n//!   todo.md's In Progress block).\n//! - Simplicity-first cut: layout + init/attach here;\n//!   alloc/free land next.\n\nuse core::marker::PhantomData;\nuse core::mem::size_of;\nuse core::sync::atomic::{AtomicU32, Ordering};\n\nuse crate::{CACHE_LINE, CacheAligned, Error};\n\n/// Layout marker written by [`Pool::init`]; rejects foreign\n/// (including ring) regions on attach.\nconst POOL_MAGIC: u32 = 0x5A43_5031; // \"ZCP1\"\n\n/// Bumped on any change to the pool region layout.\nconst POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION: u32 = 1;\n\n/// Free-stack terminator: no next free buffer.\n///\n/// - Also why `buf_count < u32::MAX`: every valid index must\n///   be distinguishable from the sentinel.\nconst NIL: u32 = u32::MAX;\n\n/// Control block at offset 0 of a pool region — two cache\n/// lines: cold geometry, then the contended free-stack head.\n///\n/// - line 0: geometry, written by [`Pool::init`] with `magic`\n///   last (`Release`), read-only thereafter. Cold: per-op\n///   paths use the handle's snapshot.\n/// - line 1: `free_head`, CAS-contended by every freer and\n///   the allocator — sole owner of its line.\n/// - Every field is atomic for the same reason as the ring\n///   [`Header`](crate::Header): a peer may be mapped at any\n///   time, and scribbles must be garbage values, never UB.\n#[repr(C)]\npub struct PoolHeader {\n    /// Layout marker ([`POOL_MAGIC`]); stored last by init\n    /// (`Release`), loaded first by attach (`Acquire`).\n    magic: AtomicU32,\n    /// Pool layout version ([`POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION`]).\n    layout_version: AtomicU32,\n    /// Buffer size in bytes — a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    buf_size: AtomicU32,\n    /// Buffer count — nonzero, `< u32::MAX` ([`NIL`]).\n    buf_count: AtomicU32,\n    /// [`CACHE_LINE`] this region was built with; attach\n    /// validates it like the rest of the geometry.\n    cache_line: AtomicU32,\n    /// Free-stack head: index of the first free buffer, or\n    /// [`NIL`] when the pool is exhausted.\n    free_head: CacheAligned<AtomicU32>,\n}\n\nconst _: () = assert!(size_of::<PoolHeader>() == 2 * CACHE_LINE);\n\n/// A validated view over a pool region.\n///\n/// - Geometry is snapshotted out of the header at\n///   init/attach: per-op paths never re-read fields a peer\n///   could scribble on. Free-stack links live in shared\n///   buffer memory and are validated at every pop instead.\npub struct Pool<'a> {\n    /// The region's control block.\n    header: &'a PoolHeader,\n    /// Base of the buffer array\n    /// (offset `size_of::<PoolHeader>()`).\n    bufs: *mut u8,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.buf_size`.\n    buf_size: u32,\n    /// Snapshot of `header.buf_count`.\n    buf_count: u32,\n    _region: PhantomData<&'a [u8]>,\n}\n\nimpl<'a> Pool<'a> {\n    /// Initialize a fresh region and return the pool over it,\n    /// with every buffer on the free-stack.\n    ///\n    /// - `buf_size` — bytes per buffer, a [`CACHE_LINE`]\n    ///   multiple.\n    /// - `buf_count` — number of buffers, nonzero and\n    ///   `< u32::MAX`.\n    /// - The region must be [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned and at\n    ///   least `size_of::<PoolHeader>() + buf_count *\n    ///   buf_size` bytes.\n    pub fn init(region: &'a mut [u8], buf_size: u32, buf_count: u32) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        validate_pool_geometry(buf_size, buf_count)?;\n        let len = region.len();\n        // Taken exactly once — same Stacked Borrows retag\n        // hazard as Ring::init.\n        let base = region.as_mut_ptr();\n        let header = pool_header_ptr(base, len)?;\n        if (len as u64) < pool_region_size(buf_size, buf_count) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the PoolHeader checked\n        // by pool_header_ptr; region is exclusively borrowed\n        // for 'a; any byte pattern is a valid PoolHeader\n        // (all-atomic fields, plain-byte padding).\n        let header = unsafe { &*header };\n        header\n            .layout_version\n            .store(POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.buf_size.store(buf_size, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header.buf_count.store(buf_count, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        header\n            .cache_line\n            .store(CACHE_LINE as u32, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + buffers.\n        let bufs = unsafe { base.add(size_of::<PoolHeader>()) };\n        let pool = Pool {\n            header,\n            bufs,\n            buf_size,\n            buf_count,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        };\n        // Link every buffer into the free-stack: i -> i + 1,\n        // last -> NIL, head -> 0.\n        for i in 0..buf_count {\n            let next = if i + 1 == buf_count { NIL } else { i + 1 };\n            pool.link_of(i).store(next, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        }\n        header.free_head.store(0, Ordering::Relaxed);\n        // Published last: a peer that pre-mapped the region\n        // must never observe the magic before the geometry it\n        // validates (same handshake as Ring::init).\n        header.magic.store(POOL_MAGIC, Ordering::Release);\n        Ok(pool)\n    }\n\n    /// Attach to a pool region another process (or an earlier\n    /// call) already initialized, validating its header.\n    ///\n    /// # Safety\n    ///\n    /// - `region` points to `len` bytes of memory that outlive\n    ///   `'a`, genuinely shared and writable (e.g. a\n    ///   `MAP_SHARED` mapping).\n    /// - At most one attached handle acts as the pool's\n    ///   allocator (single-popper contract); any handle may\n    ///   free.\n    pub unsafe fn attach(region: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<Self, Error> {\n        let header = pool_header_ptr(region, len)?;\n        // SAFETY: alignment + room for the PoolHeader checked\n        // by pool_header_ptr; caller guarantees the memory is\n        // live and shared.\n        let header = unsafe { &*header };\n        // Acquire pairs with init's Release store of magic.\n        if header.magic.load(Ordering::Acquire) != POOL_MAGIC {\n            return Err(Error::BadMagic);\n        }\n        if header.layout_version.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != POOL_LAYOUT_VERSION {\n            return Err(Error::BadLayoutVersion);\n        }\n        if header.cache_line.load(Ordering::Relaxed) != CACHE_LINE as u32 {\n            return Err(Error::BadCacheLine);\n        }\n        // Snapshot geometry once; per-op paths never re-read.\n        let buf_size = header.buf_size.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        let buf_count = header.buf_count.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n        validate_pool_geometry(buf_size, buf_count)?;\n        if (len as u64) < pool_region_size(buf_size, buf_count) {\n            return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n        }\n        // SAFETY: in bounds — len >= header + buffers.\n        let bufs = unsafe { region.add(size_of::<PoolHeader>()) };\n        Ok(Pool {\n            header,\n            bufs,\n            buf_size,\n            buf_count,\n            _region: PhantomData,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Buffer size in bytes (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_size(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.buf_size\n    }\n\n    /// Buffer count (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_count(&self) -> u32 {\n        self.buf_count\n    }\n\n    /// The intrusive next-link of buffer `idx` — its first\n    /// word, meaningful only while the buffer is free.\n    ///\n    /// - Callers pass `idx < buf_count` (validated pops /\n    ///   init's linking loop), keeping the deref in bounds.\n    fn link_of(&self, idx: u32) -> &AtomicU32 {\n        let p = self.buf_ptr(idx) as *const AtomicU32;\n        // SAFETY: idx < buf_count keeps the buffer in the\n        // region validated at init/attach; the base is\n        // cache-line aligned and buf_size is a line multiple,\n        // so the first word is 4-aligned; all peers access it\n        // as an atomic.\n        unsafe { &*p }\n    }\n\n    /// Pointer to buffer `idx`; callers pass\n    /// `idx < buf_count`.\n    fn buf_ptr(&self, idx: u32) -> *mut u8 {\n        // SAFETY: idx < buf_count, so the offset stays inside\n        // the buffer array validated at init/attach.\n        unsafe { self.bufs.add(idx as usize * self.buf_size as usize) }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the\n/// `PoolHeader` it must start with; shared by\n/// [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].\n///\n/// - Checks alignment and room for the header itself; the\n///   full-geometry length check stays with the caller (init\n///   knows the geometry from parameters, attach only after\n///   reading the header).\nfn pool_header_ptr(base: *mut u8, len: usize) -> Result<*const PoolHeader, Error> {\n    if !(base as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n        return Err(Error::Misaligned);\n    }\n    if len < size_of::<PoolHeader>() {\n        return Err(Error::TooSmall);\n    }\n    Ok(base as *const PoolHeader)\n}\n\n/// Bytes needed for a pool region with the given geometry —\n/// computed in u64 for the same 32-bit wrap reason as the\n/// ring's `region_size`.\nfn pool_region_size(buf_size: u32, buf_count: u32) -> u64 {\n    size_of::<PoolHeader>() as u64 + buf_size as u64 * buf_count as u64\n}\n\n/// Shared geometry checks for [`Pool::init`] /\n/// [`Pool::attach`].\n///\n/// - No power-of-two constraint: the free-stack never masks\n///   an index.\nfn validate_pool_geometry(buf_size: u32, buf_count: u32) -> Result<(), Error> {\n    if buf_size == 0 || !(buf_size as usize).is_multiple_of(CACHE_LINE) {\n        return Err(Error::BadBufSize);\n    }\n    if buf_count == 0 || buf_count == NIL {\n        return Err(Error::BadBufCount);\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}\n"
}
```

  "new_string": "/// Errors from region validation — [`Ring::init`] /\n/// [`Ring::attach`] and [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].\n#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum Error {\n    /// Region is not [`CACHE_LINE`]-aligned.\n    Misaligned,\n    /// Region is smaller than header + slots/buffers.\n    TooSmall,\n    /// Slot size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE`] multiple.\n    BadSlotSize,\n    /// Capacity is zero, not a power of two, or `> 2^31`.\n    BadCapacity,\n    /// Pool: buffer size is zero or not a [`CACHE_LINE`]\n    /// multiple.\n    BadBufSize,\n    /// Pool: buffer count is zero or `u32::MAX` (the\n    /// free-stack NIL sentinel).\n    BadBufCount,\n    /// Attach: magic mismatch — not a region of the expected\n    /// kind.\n    BadMagic,\n    /// Attach: layout version mismatch.\n    BadLayoutVersion,\n    /// Attach: region built with a different [`CACHE_LINE`].\n    BadCacheLine,\n}"

> AGENT

`0.6.0-1 feat: message pool layout + init/attach` is ready to commit. What landed:

**`src/pool.rs`** (new, ~250 lines) — the pool's region layer, deliberately mirroring the ring's discipline so the two read as siblings:

- `PoolHeader`, two cache lines: line 0 is cold geometry (`magic` "ZCP1", `layout_version` 1, `buf_size`, `buf_count`, `cache_line`), line 1 is the `free_head` alone — it's the CAS-contended word, so it owns its line. All fields atomic, same hostile-peer rationale as the ring. Const-asserted at 128 bytes.
- **Zero per-buffer overhead**: the intrusive next-link is the first word of a *free* buffer (meaningful only while free); an allocated buffer carries no header at all. `NIL = u32::MAX` terminates the stack, which is why `buf_count < u32::MAX` is a geometry rule. No power-of-two constraint — a stack never masks.
- `Pool::init` — validates geometry (u64 region-size math, same 32-bit wrap guard as the ring), links every buffer `i → i+1`, head = 0, magic-last `Release` handshake. `unsafe Pool::attach` — magic `Acquire` first, then layout version, cache line, geometry snapshot; its safety contract states the single-popper rule: at most one attached handle allocates, any handle frees.
- `buf_size()` / `buf_count()` accessors; private `link_of`/`buf_ptr` helpers that `-2`'s alloc/free will reuse.

**`src/lib.rs`** — `pool` module wired in and re-exported; `Error` gains `BadBufSize` / `BadBufCount` (one shared error enum, doc comment updated).

**Notes/version** — ladder `-1` flipped `(done)`; your flagged backfill done: `Commits: [[15]]` on the cycle's chores section with the now-permanent `0.6.0-0` SHA `1d6d3889…`; version-of-record `0.6.0-1`.

Validation: fmt, clippy `-D warnings`, cargo test, and full Miri all pass (tests exercising the pool itself come at `-3` per the ladder). Review when ready.

> DEVELOPER

In pool.rs PoolHeader what is cache_line?

> AGENT

It's the recorded cache-line size the region was built with — a copy of the crate's `CACHE_LINE` constant (currently 64) stored into the header at `Pool::init`, and checked by `Pool::attach`, which returns `Error::BadCacheLine` on mismatch (src/pool.rs:169).

Why it's worth a header word: `CACHE_LINE` isn't just a performance hint here, it's a **layout parameter**. It determines the header's size and padding (`CacheAligned` wrappers), the buffer-size granularity rule, and buffer alignment. Two processes sharing a pool region must agree on it or they'd compute different offsets for the same bytes — silent corruption, not slowdown.

Today every build uses 64, so the check can never fail between honest peers. It exists for the future in the Ideas list: per-target or configurable `CACHE_LINE` (128 for Apple M-series false-sharing pitch, smaller for cache-less MCUs). When that lands, a build compiled with 128 attaching to a region built with 64 gets a clean `BadCacheLine` error instead of undetected layout disagreement. The ring's `Header` has carried the same field since its layout v2 — the pool inherits the discipline (assume nothing, verify at the boundary).

> DEVELOPER

free_head_idx how about:  next_buf_idx. Push back it you don't like it

> AGENT

I'll push back — I'd keep `free_head_idx`. Three reasons:

1. **`next_buf_idx` describes only the pop side.** From the allocator's view it is "the next buffer you'll get," but from a freer's view it's *where you push*. The field serves both operations; "head of the free-stack" is the neutral name that fits both, while "next buf" reads like a bump-allocator cursor marching through the array in sequence — which actively misleads about the data structure behind it.

2. **It collides with the per-buffer links.** Each free buffer's first word is already its *next*-link (`link_of` — "index of the next free buffer"). If the header field is also named "next," the design's two distinct roles — the head of the stack versus a node's next pointer — share one word, and the code stops telling you which is which.

3. **Convention carries information.** "Free head" is the canonical name for this exact thing in free-list/Treiber-stack literature; a reader who's seen any allocator recognizes `free_head_idx` on sight, including the LIFO implication that `next_buf_idx` erases.

The name spells out the structure: it's the head (so a stack), of the free list (so only free buffers), holding an index (your earlier improvement). If the itch is that "head" is opaque, `first_free_idx` would be my second choice — it keeps "free" and stays direction-neutral — but `free_head_idx` says the most per character. Keep it?

> DEVELOPER

link_of how about get_next_buf_idx or next_buf_idx, at some point we'll need the ref pointer too?

> AGENT

`next_buf_idx` works — dropping the `get_` per Rust convention (getters aren't `get_`-prefixed). And it's nicely symmetric with the header's `free_head_idx`: both are `AtomicU32` cells holding a buffer index. Note it returns the atomic *cell* (`&AtomicU32`), not the value — the doc comment keeps saying so.

On the ref pointer: that's already there as the private `buf_ptr(idx)`. It stays raw on purpose — in `-2` the alloc guard will mint `&mut T` from it per access via `Deref`, exactly like the ring's `WriteSlot` (the raw-pointer-not-reference-field lesson Miri taught in 0.3.0-6), with `check_type` validating `T` against `buf_size`.

> DEVELOPER

Looking at line 137 of pool.rs I read `pool.header.free_head_idx` and possibly freeing what ever is at head, a buffer. It doesn't feel right. Again ur welcome to push back

> AGENT

No pushback this time — you've found a real flaw my earlier defense missed: **"free" is a verb as well as an adjective.** `pool.header.free_head_idx.store(...)` genuinely parses as "free the head idx" — an action — and at a store site that misreading is live, exactly as you experienced. My earlier case was for keeping the word "free"; it didn't consider the verb trap.

The fix is a name where "free" can only be an adjective: **`first_free_idx`** — "index of the first free buffer." No verb reading ("first" blocks it), keeps "free" and "idx", and it's a familiar allocator idiom. It was my stated second choice earlier and the verb problem promotes it to first.

> DEVELOPER

Why attach after init, why not have init imply attaching?

> AGENT

`init` doesn't need a follow-up attach — it already implies it. `Pool::init` returns a fully usable `Pool` handle (same for `Ring::init`): the initializing side never calls `attach` at all. The two functions aren't sequential steps; they're **two doors into the same handle** for two different parties:

- **`init`** — for the region's creator. It has the memory as `&mut [u8]`, so the borrow checker *proves* exclusive access: nobody else can be watching while the header is half-written. That proof is what lets `init` be a safe function, and it returns the ready handle directly.
- **`attach`** — for everyone else: the other process (or a later re-mapping) joining a region someone else already initialized. It can't take `&mut [u8]`, because a genuinely shared `MAP_SHARED` mapping is never exclusively borrowed — a peer may be writing at any moment. Hence the raw pointer, the header validation (magic/version/geometry, which `init` doesn't need — it just wrote those), and the `unsafe` contract about mapping liveness.

So the asymmetry in signatures is the design: `init` = create + attach fused, safe by exclusivity; `attach` = join, unsafe because only the caller can vouch for the mapping.

The tempting third shape — one `open_or_create` that inits if the region looks fresh, attaches otherwise — is the one I'd argue against. "Looks fresh" means checking `magic`, and two processes racing through that check both conclude "fresh" and both initialize: a TOCTOU race the current handshake deliberately doesn't have to handle, because *who initializes* is the caller's topology decision (embedded: startup code before spawning; Linux: the creator of the shm fd inits before passing it). Making that race safe would need a CAS claim on `magic` and defined loser semantics — machinery that buys convenience, not capability. If it's ever wanted, it belongs as a third door built on the existing two, not a replacement for them.

> DEVELOPER

How about "use" instead of "attach"?

> AGENT

`use` is off the table on hard grounds: it's a Rust keyword, so the method would have to be written `r#use` — `Pool::r#use(...)` at every call site — which no API should inflict.

Setting the keyword aside, I'd still defend `attach` over its legal cousins:

- It names the relationship accurately: the region already exists and is live; this side is *attaching itself* to it. That's exactly the POSIX shared-memory verb too (`shmat` = "shared memory attach"), so systems programmers arrive pre-trained.
- `open` (iceoryx2's choice) implies a resource being located and acquired — but we take a pointer to memory the caller already mapped; nothing is opened.
- `join` reads socially fine but has no systems lineage; `map` is wrong (the caller did the mapping; we validate it).

And there's a repo-specific cost: `attach` is load-bearing across the existing surface — `Ring::attach`, the design doc's init/attach handshake sections, `BadMagic`/`BadCacheLine` doc references, the "attach validates it" idiom in comments. Renaming just the pool's door would break the ring/pool symmetry we've been deliberately maintaining; renaming both is a wide diff for a synonym swap.

Keep `attach`?

> DEVELOPER

good reason, attach for now but nothing is in stone :)
How do I get ownership of a buf so I can use it?

> AGENT

Today you can't — that's deliberately the next rung: `0.6.0-2 feat: message pool alloc/free`. The `-1` on the table only builds and validates the region. But since the API shape is exactly what this cycle exists to hone, here's the shape I intend for `-2`:

```rust
// allocator side (the one owning handle):
let mut msg: BufSlot<'_, MyMsg> = pool.alloc::<MyMsg>()?;   // pops first_free_idx, validated
msg.field = 42;                                             // DerefMut, in place
// ... hold it as long as you like, alloc more meanwhile ...
msg.free();                                                 // pushes back onto the free-stack
```

- **`alloc` pops the free-stack**: load `first_free_idx`, bounds-check it (untrusted), read that buffer's `next_buf_idx`, bounds-check *that*, CAS the head over. Returns `Err(Exhausted)` on `NIL`. Typed via the same zerocopy bounds and `check_type` as the ring's slots.
- **The guard borrows the pool *shared*, not `&mut`** — this is the load-bearing decision. The ring's `WriteSlot` holds `&mut Producer`, so one reservation at a time; if `BufSlot` did that, we'd have rebuilt the exact coupling this cycle exists to remove. Instead many `BufSlot`s live concurrently; per-buffer exclusivity comes from the protocol (each pop yields a distinct index), not the borrow checker.
- **Ownership semantics**: the guard *is* your ownership token. `free(self)` returns the buffer (a CAS push any handle/process may do); dropping without freeing leaks the buffer until the pool is re-initialized — documented, like the ring's abandon-on-drop but with the opposite consequence. "Send it" (the descriptor-queue cycle) will later consume the guard into a bare index and the receiver will reconstruct access on its side.
- `alloc` takes `&mut self` on the allocator handle to enforce single-popper in-process; `free` needs only `&self`.

That last asymmetry echoes your original insight: getting is a private act of the one allocator, releasing is open to the world.

If that shape reads right, the natural order is: commit `-1` as reviewed, then I implement `-2` on top. Shall I write the `-1` description?

> DEVELOPER

yes, write the -1 description

feat: message pool layout + init/attach

Message pool region layer, mirroring the ring's discipline:
all-atomic two-line PoolHeader (cold geometry; contended
first_free_idx alone on line 1), magic-last Release/Acquire
init/attach handshake, geometry snapshot so per-op paths
never re-read peer-writable fields. The free-stack is
intrusive with zero per-buffer overhead: a free buffer's
first word is its next_buf_idx link; alloc/free land next.

  buf_size, buf_count, cache_line_size, first_free_idx),
  Pool::init (validates geometry in u64, links buffers
  i -> i+1, last -> NIL, head 0), unsafe Pool::attach
  (single-popper contract in its safety docs), buf_size /
  buf_count accessors, private next_buf_idx / buf_ptr
  helpers for the coming alloc/free
- src/lib.rs
  - pool module wired in; Pool, PoolHeader re-exported
  - Error gains BadBufSize / BadBufCount; doc updated to

    symmetry (field rename only, no layout change)
- notes/ring-buffer-design.md: cache_line_size rename synced

- Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock: version-of-record 0.6.0-1
```

  "old_string": "    /// Buffer size in bytes (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_size(&self) -> u32 {",
  "new_string": "    /// Pop the first free buffer off the free-stack as an\n    /// owned [`BufSlot`], or [`Exhausted`].\n    ///\n    /// - `&mut self` is the in-process single-popper token;\n    ///   cross-process, at most one attached handle allocates\n    ///   (see [`Pool::attach`]). The guard does **not** borrow\n    ///   the pool — any number of allocated buffers may be\n    ///   live at once; per-buffer exclusivity comes from the\n    ///   free-stack protocol (each pop yields a distinct\n    ///   index), not the borrow checker.\n    /// - Validated pop: the head and its next-link live in\n    ///   peer-writable memory, so both are bounds-checked;\n    ///   corruption degrades to `Exhausted` (mirroring the\n    ///   ring's fail-toward-`Full` occupancy check), never to\n    ///   an out-of-bounds buffer.\n    /// - A racing free moves the head and the CAS retries;\n    ///   with a single popper an in-flight head node cannot be\n    ///   removed underneath us, so there is no ABA hazard.\n    pub fn alloc<T>(&mut self) -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, Exhausted>\n    where\n        T: FromBytes + IntoBytes + KnownLayout,\n    {\n        check_type::<T>(self.buf_size);\n        loop {\n            // Acquire pairs with free's Release CAS: seeing a\n            // head index means seeing that buffer's next-link\n            // store (and the freer's last writes) too.\n            let head = self.header.first_free_idx.load(Ordering::Acquire);\n            if head == NIL {\n                return Err(Exhausted);\n            }\n            if head >= self.buf_count {\n                return Err(Exhausted);\n            }\n            let next = self.next_buf_idx(head).load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n            if next != NIL && next >= self.buf_count {\n                return Err(Exhausted);\n            }\n            if self\n                .header\n                .first_free_idx\n                .compare_exchange(head, next, Ordering::Acquire, Ordering::Relaxed)\n                .is_ok()\n            {\n                return Ok(BufSlot {\n                    header: self.header,\n                    buf: self.buf_ptr(head),\n                    idx: head,\n                    _slot: PhantomData,\n                });\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Buffer size in bytes (geometry snapshot).\n    pub fn buf_size(&self) -> u32 {"
}

  "old_string": "/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the\n/// `PoolHeader` it must start with; shared by\n/// [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`].",
  "new_string": "/// An allocated buffer, owned until [`free`](BufSlot::free):\n/// `DerefMut` to use it as a `T` in place.\n///\n/// - Does not borrow the [`Pool`] — hold any number, as long\n///   as you like; getting a buffer implies nothing about\n///   when (or whether) it is sent or freed.\n/// - Dropping without `free` leaks the buffer until the pool\n///   is re-initialized (the abandon analog of the ring's\n///   guards, with the opposite consequence).\npub struct BufSlot<'p, T> {\n    /// The pool's control block (for the free CAS).\n    header: &'p PoolHeader,\n    /// Base of the owned buffer. Raw, and references are\n    /// minted per access — same aliasing rationale as the\n    /// ring guards.\n    buf: *mut u8,\n    /// This buffer's index (the value free pushes).\n    idx: u32,\n    /// The guard acts as a `&mut T` into the region.\n    _slot: PhantomData<&'p mut T>,\n}\n\n// SAFETY: the guard owns its buffer exclusively (the pop\n// removed it from every shared structure); free's CAS is the\n// only shared-state touch and is properly ordered.\nunsafe impl<T: Send> Send for BufSlot<'_, T> {}\n\nimpl<T> Deref for BufSlot<'_, T> {\n    type Target = T;\n    /// Read access to the buffer as a `T`.\n    fn deref(&self) -> &T {\n        // SAFETY: buf is in-bounds and cache-line aligned\n        // (validated geometry), any byte pattern is a valid T\n        // (FromBytes bound at alloc), and the pop gave this\n        // guard sole ownership until free.\n        unsafe { &*(self.buf as *const T) }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> DerefMut for BufSlot<'_, T> {\n    /// Write access to the buffer as a `T`.\n    fn deref_mut(&mut self) -> &mut T {\n        // SAFETY: as in deref; &mut self gives exclusivity of\n        // the minted reference.\n        unsafe { &mut *(self.buf as *mut T) }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<T> BufSlot<'_, T> {\n    /// Push the buffer back onto the pool's free-stack.\n    ///\n    /// - Any holder may free — this is the free-stack's MPSC\n    ///   push side; only allocation is single-popper.\n    /// - The CAS retries only when another free (or the\n    ///   allocator's pop) moves the head first.\n    pub fn free(self) {\n        // The buffer's first word becomes its next-link again.\n        let link = self.buf as *const AtomicU32;\n        // SAFETY: buf is in-bounds and 4-aligned (cache-line\n        // aligned base); the guard still owns the buffer, and\n        // all peers access this word as an atomic.\n        let link = unsafe { &*link };\n        loop {\n            let head = self.header.first_free_idx.load(Ordering::Relaxed);\n            link.store(head, Ordering::Relaxed);\n            // Release pairs with alloc's Acquire loads: the\n            // popper that sees idx also sees the link store\n            // above (and this holder's last buffer writes).\n            if self\n                .header\n                .first_free_idx\n                .compare_exchange(head, self.idx, Ordering::Release, Ordering::Relaxed)\n                .is_ok()\n            {\n                return;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Validate a region base pointer and cast it to the\n/// `PoolHeader` it must start with; shared by\n/// [`Pool::init`] / [`Pool::attach`]."
}

> AGENT

`0.6.0-2 feat: message pool alloc/free` is ready to commit — the API is exactly the shape we agreed:

**`Pool::alloc<T>(&mut self) -> Result<BufSlot<'a, T>, Exhausted>`** (src/pool.rs:203)
- Validated Treiber pop: head `Acquire`-loaded and bounds-checked, its next-link bounds-checked too, then CAS the head over; corruption fails toward `Exhausted` (the ring's fail-toward-`Full` philosophy), never an out-of-bounds buffer. A racing free just retries the CAS; single popper means no ABA.
- `&mut self` is only the in-process single-popper token — the returned guard borrows the *region* lifetime, not the pool handle, so allocs stack up freely.

**`BufSlot<T>`** — the ownership token: `Deref`/`DerefMut` mint `&T`/`&mut T` per access from a raw pointer (the ring guards' Miri-taught aliasing pattern), `free(self)` is the MPSC push (link store then `Release` CAS, retry on contention), dropping without `free` leaks the buffer (documented). `Send` when `T: Send`, so a holder can free from another thread — the topology the design calls for. `zerocopy` bounds + `check_type` against `buf_size` as everywhere else.

If you'd rather see the tests before committing this, I can pull `-3` forward; otherwise review and I'll write the description.

The tests\nexercise exactly what this model permits; anything outside\nit is a contract violation even where the compiler cannot\nreject it.\n\n**Roles.** Roles are per *object*, and one thread may hold\nseveral roles across objects (a typical sender is one pool's\nallocator and one ring's producer):\n\n- **Ring** — exactly one producer and one consumer, ever\n  (SPSC): in-process, `split()` hands out each endpoint\n  once; cross-process, one producing and one consuming\n  process by contract. An endpoint reserves at most one\n  slot at a time (guard holds the endpoint borrow).\n- **Pool** — exactly one *allocator* at a time (the\n  single-popper contract on [`Pool::attach`]; in-process,\n  `alloc` takes `&mut self`), and **any number of freers**:\n  whoever holds a `BufSlot` may free it, from any thread or\n  process attached to the pool.\n\n**Buffer lifecycle.** A pool buffer is always in exactly one\nstate, with one party allowed to touch it:\n\n- **free** — on the free-stack; the pool protocol owns it.\n  Nobody reads or writes it except through alloc/free\n  machinery (its first word is the next-link).\n- **allocated** — popped by `alloc`, owned by the `BufSlot`\n  holder: exclusive read/write through the guard, held as\n  long as desired, moved between threads freely (`Send`).\n  No other party — including the pool — may touch the\n  bytes.\n- **in-flight** — *placeholder, vacant this cycle*: after a\n  send over a descriptor queue the sender must no longer\n  touch the buffer and the receiver owns it on receipt.\n  Until descriptor queues exist there is no in-flight\n  state.\n- **freed** — `BufSlot::free` pushes it back; ownership\n  returns to the pool protocol the instant the CAS lands.\n  Use-after-free of the guard is unrepresentable (`free`\n  consumes it); dropping without `free` leaks the buffer.\n\n**What \"send\" means this cycle.** Transferring a message =\nmoving its `BufSlot` (an ordinary Rust move; `Send` lets it\ncross threads). The ring and the pool do not yet compose:\na `BufSlot` cannot travel through a ring slot, and no API\nreconstructs buffer access from a bare index — both arrive\nwith the descriptor-queue cycle. Ring messages this cycle\nare by-value `T`s in slots, as before.\n\n**Trust.** Unchanged from the ring: every word in shared\nmemory is untrusted input. The pool validates the head and\nnext-link at every pop and fails toward `Exhausted`; a\nhostile peer can degrade service (garbage messages, lost\nbuffers, spurious exhaustion), never cause UB on this side.\n\n### Overflow FIFO (future)"

> AGENT

`0.6.0-3 docs: message pool usage model` is ready to commit — and since the point of this rung is agreement, here's the model it pins down (new `### Usage model: roles and buffer lifecycle` in the messaging-layer section):

**Roles** (per object; one thread may hold several across objects — a typical sender is a pool's allocator plus a ring's producer):
- **Ring**: exactly one producer, one consumer, ever; one reserved slot at a time per endpoint.
- **Pool**: exactly one allocator at a time (single-popper), any number of freers — whoever holds a `BufSlot` may free it from any thread or process.

**Buffer lifecycle** — always exactly one state, one permitted toucher:
- **free** — the pool protocol owns it; its first word is the next-link; nobody else touches it.
- **allocated** — the `BufSlot` holder has exclusive read/write, indefinitely, movable across threads.
- **in-flight** — *explicitly documented as vacant this cycle*: it exists in the model so the state machine is honest, but only descriptor queues will populate it.
- **freed** — ownership snaps back to the pool at the free-CAS; use-after-free is unrepresentable (`free` consumes the guard), drop-without-free leaks.

**"Send" this cycle** = moving the `BufSlot` (ordinary Rust move / `Send`). Stated plainly: ring and pool do not yet compose — no `BufSlot` through a ring slot, no reconstruction from a bare index; ring messages remain by-value `T`s.

**Trust** — unchanged doctrine: shared words are untrusted input; hostile peers degrade service, never cause UB.

The section intro also now records the pool half as as-built (0.6.0 cycle) rather than "not yet built." Bookkeeping rode along: `[[17]]` backfilled with the `-2` SHA, ladder flipped, version `0.6.0-3`.

The `-4` tests will be written directly against this model: roles enforced, lifecycle transitions exercised (including cross-thread free and exhaustion), nothing outside it. If the model reads right, I'll write the description.

notes/README.md
README.md
# zc-ring-x1

Zero copy ring buffer experiment 1

This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
a bot to help develop the code.

The beginnings of a tool to help is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)

## Overview

A `no_std` SPSC (single-producer / single-consumer) ring buffer
over a caller-provided memory region, moving typed messages with
zero copying at the boundary — the producer writes through a
`&mut T` directly into buffer memory, the consumer reads through
a `&T` in place. Typed views are validated casts via
[zerocopy](https://docs.rs/zerocopy); the same `#[repr(C)]`
layout works between threads and, via shared memory, between
processes. The full design — requirements, memory layout, index
scheme, API, validation — lives in
[notes/ring-buffer-design.md](notes/ring-buffer-design.md), kept
in sync with `src/`.

- M slots × N bytes, both caller-chosen: M a power of two, N a
  cache-line multiple, every slot cache-line aligned.
- Free-running `AtomicU32` indices, masked only at slot access;
  no sacrificial slot; lock-free with Acquire/Release pairing.
- Guard API: `reserve_slot::<T>()` on either endpoint — write →
  `commit()` on the producer side, read → `release()` on the
  consumer side; dropping a guard abandons cleanly.
- IPC-ready: one contiguous region, no internal pointers, an
  init/attach handshake (magic published last, Release), and an
  all-atomic header so a misbehaving peer can corrupt data but
  never cause UB.
- An app-owned scratch line in the header (`user()` on both
  endpoints, 16 `AtomicU32`s): the crate zeroes it at init and
  never touches it again — a shared-memory home for app wakeup
  protocols; the crate itself never blocks or spins.
- Validated by tests (including a threaded stress and a
  u32-index-wrap proof) and [Miri](https://github.com/rust-lang/miri)
```

  u32-index-wrap proof) and [Miri](https://github.com/rust-lang/miri)
  on the non-threaded suite.

```rust
use zc_ring_x1::Ring;
use zerocopy::{FromBytes, Immutable, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};

#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout, Immutable)]
#[repr(C)]
struct Msg {
    seq: u64,
    val: u64,
}

// Cache-line-aligned region: 256 B header + 4 slots × 64 B.
#[repr(C, align(64))]
struct Region([u8; 512]);
let mut region = Region([0; 512]);

let (mut producer, mut consumer) =
    Ring::init(&mut region.0, 64, 4).unwrap().split();

let mut slot = producer.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
slot.seq = 1;
slot.val = 42;
slot.commit(); // publish to the consumer

let msg = consumer.reserve_slot::<Msg>().unwrap();
assert_eq!(msg.val, 42);
msg.release(); // slot is free for reuse
```

Status: an experiment. SPSC only; attaching to an existing
shared-memory region is `unsafe` (see `Ring::attach`); planned
hardening and follow-ons are tracked in
[notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md).

## Testing

- `cargo test` — the full suite, including a threaded stress
  test and a u32-index-wrap test.
- `cargo run --example readme` — the Overview example above
  (committed as [examples/readme.rs](examples/readme.rs) so
  `cargo clippy --all-targets` keeps it compiling against the
  real API).
- `cargo +nightly miri test` — the full suite under
  [Miri](https://github.com/rust-lang/miri), which checks the
  `unsafe` code against the memory model; it has caught two
  real Stacked Borrows bugs here (an init retag in 0.3.0-4, a
  guard aliasing race in 0.3.0-6 — the latter only visible
  with the threaded test included). One-time setup:
  `rustup component add --toolchain nightly miri`. The
  threaded stress test runs a reduced message count under
  Miri (interpreted spin loops are slow).

## Releasing

**`cargo install` lockfile gotcha.** If you install your project's
binary with `cargo install --path .`, also pass `--locked`. By
default `cargo install` ignores `Cargo.lock` and re-resolves
dependencies from scratch, so it can silently build the binary from
a different — possibly broken — dependency graph than `cargo build`
/ `cargo test` ran against.

| command | reads `Cargo.lock`? | re-resolves? |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `cargo build` / `test` / `run`  | yes     | only if `Cargo.toml` changed |
| `cargo build --locked` (etc.)   | yes     | never; errors if it would need to |
| `cargo install` (default)       | **no**  | always, from scratch |
| `cargo install --locked`        | yes     | never; errors if it would need to |

There's no stable cargo config knob to make `--locked` the default
for `cargo install`, so the discipline lives in the per-commit cargo
cycle and shell aliases (e.g. `alias ci='cargo install --locked'`).

The trade-off is predictable-as-tested (`--locked`) vs picking up
upstream fixes via fresh resolves (default). Either is defensible —
pick what fits your project, then edit this section and the
per-commit cargo cycle to match. Background:
[cargo#7169](https://github.com/rust-lang/cargo/issues/7169),
[cargo#9436](https://github.com/rust-lang/cargo/issues/9436).

## jj Tips for Git Users

This project uses [Jujutsu (jj)](https://docs.jj-vcs.dev/latest/)
alongside git. New to jj? See
[Steve Klabnik](https://github.com/steveklabnik)'s
[Jujutsu tutorial](https://steveklabnik.github.io/jujutsu-tutorial).

Repo-specific how-tos — initial commit, pushing, modifying and
force-pushing a commit, revsets, and a useful-commands reference —
live in [notes/jj-tips.md](notes/jj-tips.md).

## Cross-repo Linking with Git Trailers

Commits in each repo use [git trailers](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-interpret-trailers)
to cross-reference their counterpart in the other repo via an
`ochid` (Other Change ID) trailer — the defining mechanism of the
dual-repo convention. For the full definition — trailer syntax,
the example shape, per-commit mechanics, and `.vc-config.toml` —
see
[Cross-repo linking (ochid trailers)](AGENTS.md#cross-repo-linking-ochid-trailers).

## Contributing

Bot-following workflow, commit conventions, and code style are
canonical in [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) (which `CLAUDE.md` imports)
and [notes/cycle-protocol.md](notes/cycle-protocol.md):

- [Cycle numbering](notes/cycle-protocol.md#numbering) — the
  `X.Y.Z-N` phase-suffix convention (Preparation / Work /
  Close-out).
- [Commit description](notes/cycle-protocol.md#commit-description)
  — Conventional Commits title rules (no version suffix,
  distinct per step) and per-repo body shape.
- [Per-commit flow](notes/cycle-protocol.md#per-commit-flow) —
  the cargo cycle plus the Work and Commit-Description review
  gates.
- [Code Conventions](AGENTS.md#code-conventions) — doc comments on
  every file / fn / method, `// OK: …` on `unwrap*` calls,
  ask-on-ambiguity, stuck detection.

Task tracking and release details live under [notes/](notes/):
near-term tasks in [notes/todo.md](notes/todo.md), per-release
details in `notes/chores/chores-*.md`, and notes-specific
formatting rules in [notes/README.md](notes/README.md).

Design and roadmap:\n[Messaging layer: pools and descriptor queues](notes/ring-buffer-design.md#messaging-layer-pools-and-descriptor-queues).\n\n- Intrusive LIFO free-stack with zero per-buffer overhead: a\n  *free* buffer's first word is its next-link; an allocated\n  buffer carries no header at all.\n- One allocator, any freers: `alloc` is a single-popper\n  Treiber pop (`&mut self`, no ABA); `BufSlot::free` is an\n  MPSC push callable wherever the guard was moved.\n- `BufSlot` does not borrow the pool — any number of\n  allocated buffers live at once; dropping without `free`\n  leaks the buffer (documented, like the ring's abandon but\n  with the opposite consequence).\n- Free-stack words are untrusted shared memory: pops are\n  bounds-checked and corruption degrades to `Exhausted`,\n  never out-of-bounds access.\n- Same init/attach handshake, all-atomic header, and\n  hostile-peer posture as the ring.\n\n```rust\nuse zc_ring_x1::Pool;\nuse zerocopy::{FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout};\n\n#[derive(FromBytes, IntoBytes, KnownLayout)]\n#[repr(C)]\nstruct Msg {\n    seq: u64,\n    val: u64,\n}\n\n// Cache-line-aligned region: 128 B header + 4 bufs × 64 B.\n#[repr(C, align(64))]\nstruct Region([u8; 384]);\nlet mut region = Region([0; 384]);\n\nlet mut pool = Pool::init(&mut region.0, 64, 4).unwrap();\n\nlet mut a = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap();\nlet mut b = pool.alloc::<Msg>().unwrap(); // many live at once\na.seq = 1;\nb.seq = 2;\nb.free(); // any order, from any thread the guard moved to\na.free();\n```\n\n## Usage model: roles and buffer lifecycle\n\nWho may do what, per object and per buffer state. The tests\nexercise exactly what this model permits; anything outside it\nis a contract violation even where the compiler cannot reject\nit.\n\n**Roles.** Roles are per *object*, and one thread may hold\nseveral roles across objects (a typical sender is one pool's\nallocator and one ring's producer):\n\n- **Ring** — exactly one producer and one consumer, ever\n  (SPSC): in-process, `split()` hands out each endpoint\n  once; cross-process, one producing and one consuming\n  process by contract. An endpoint reserves at most one\n  slot at a time (guard holds the endpoint borrow).\n- **Pool** — exactly one *allocator* at a time (the\n  single-popper contract on `Pool::attach`; in-process,\n  `alloc` takes `&mut self`), and **any number of freers**:\n  whoever holds a `BufSlot` may free it, from any thread or\n  process attached to the pool.\n\n**Buffer lifecycle.** A pool buffer is always in exactly one\nstate, with one party allowed to touch it:\n\n- **free** — on the free-stack; the pool protocol owns it.\n  Nobody reads or writes it except through alloc/free\n  machinery (its first word is the next-link).\n- **allocated** — popped by `alloc`, owned by the `BufSlot`\n  holder: exclusive read/write through the guard, held as\n  long as desired, moved between threads freely (`Send`).\n  No other party — including the pool — may touch the\n  bytes.\n- **in-flight** — *placeholder, vacant this cycle*: after a\n  send over a descriptor queue the sender must no longer\n  touch the buffer and the receiver owns it on receipt.\n  Until descriptor queues exist there is no in-flight\n  state.\n- **freed** — `BufSlot::free` pushes it back; ownership\n  returns to the pool protocol the instant the CAS lands.\n  Use-after-free of the guard is unrepresentable (`free`\n  consumes it); dropping without `free` leaks the buffer.\n\n**What \"send\" means this cycle.** Transferring a message =\nmoving its `BufSlot` (an ordinary Rust move; `Send` lets it\ncross threads). The ring and the pool do not yet compose:\na `BufSlot` cannot travel through a ring slot, and no API\nreconstructs buffer access from a bare index — both arrive\nwith the descriptor-queue cycle. Ring messages this cycle\nare by-value `T`s in slots, as before.\n\n**Trust.** Unchanged from the ring: every word in shared\nmemory is untrusted input. The pool validates the head and\nnext-link at every pop and fails toward `Exhausted`; a\nhostile peer can degrade service (garbage messages, lost\nbuffers, spurious exhaustion), never cause UB on this side.\n\n## Testing"
}
```

> AGENT

**Drop semantics**: they're *available* — I chose not to use them, and it's worth deciding deliberately since this cycle exists to hone the API. `free` is just a CAS push, perfectly legal inside a `Drop` impl; auto-free-on-drop (RAII, iceoryx2's choice for its samples) is implementable today. I went explicit for two reasons:

1. **Symmetry with the ring guards** — `commit`/`release` are explicit acts and drop means "do nothing" (abandon). A `BufSlot` that frees on drop would make drop *mean something* in one guard type and nothing in the others.
2. **The future send path** — descriptor-queue send will consume the guard *without* freeing (ownership goes in-flight). With a `Drop` impl, `free(self)` and `send(self)` both need the `ManuallyDrop`/`mem::forget` dance to move fields out past the destructor — done all the time, but machinery.

The honest counterweight: drop-means-leak is the harsher default for users — forget a `free` on an early-return path and the buffer is gone until re-init, silently. RAII would make the leak impossible and demote explicit `free` to an optimization. Reasonable people pick either; my lean is to keep explicit-free while the API is this young (leaks are visible in tests; `ManuallyDrop` machinery is easier to add than remove), and revisit when send lands. But if the leak-on-drop footgun bothers you more than the asymmetry-with-ring argument, now is the cheapest moment to flip it — say the word.

  "new_string": "- `alloc::<T>()` returns a `BufSlot<T>` — the ownership\n  token for one buffer, `Deref`/`DerefMut` to `T` in place.\n  The pop is single-popper Treiber (`&mut self`, no ABA):\n  one allocator per pool.\n- `BufSlot::free` is an MPSC push: whichever thread or\n  process ends up holding the guard may free it.\n- `Pool::init` creates and validates a fresh region;\n  `Pool::attach` joins one initialized elsewhere — `unsafe`\n  like `Ring::attach` (the caller vouches for the mapping),\n  and its contract carries the single-allocator rule: at\n  most one attached handle allocates, any handle frees."
}

The ring and the pool do not yet compose:\na `BufSlot` cannot travel through a ring slot, and no API\nreconstructs buffer access from a bare index — both arrive\nwith the descriptor-queue cycle. Ring messages this cycle\nare by-value `T`s in slots, as before.\n\n**Where this is heading.** The ring alone is a building\nblock; the payoff is the two composed — the ring carrying\ntiny descriptors while payloads stay put in pool buffers.\nIllustrative only (this API does not exist yet; names will\nchange):\n\n```rust,ignore\n// Sender: pool allocator + ring producer.\nlet mut msg = pool.alloc::<Msg>()?; // get a message\nmsg.val = 42;                       // fill it in place\nproducer.send(msg)?;                // consumes the BufSlot;\n                                    // only its index travels\n                                    // through the ring\n\n// Receiver: ring consumer + freer (other thread or process).\nlet desc = consumer.reserve_slot::<Desc>()?;\nlet msg = pool.resolve::<Msg>(&desc)?; // zero-copy view\n//                  ... read msg ...\nmsg.free();     // buffer back to its pool\ndesc.release(); // ring slot free for the next descriptor\n```\n\nOne allocation's bytes are written once and never copied —\nnot by send, not by receive, not across the process\nboundary."
}
